map is $\widehat{\mathbb Z}\to\mathbb Z_p$.
In $D/p$, the nonzero basis vector $\gamma_1=T$ satisfies
$\gamma_1^p=p!\gamma_p=0$.

For a generator $b$ of $\mathbb Z$, collection is
$b^ab^c=b^{a+c}$, so the Hall polynomial is $a+c$.
All brackets vanish in the abelian $\mathbb Z_p$ Lie
algebra, giving the same BCH law. These direct rank-one
calculations require no general Lazard hypothesis.
The envelope maps to its additive finite stage, but
has the additional profinite generic component factor
and the constant special fibres of E.14.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.18}{Proved}{The marked orientation-preserving modular quotient}
Put

$$
\lambda_0=\sigma\rho\sigma^{-1}\rho=
 \begin{pmatrix}-1&2\\0&-1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \lambda_1=\rho\lambda_0\rho^{-1}=
 \begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\-2&-1\end{pmatrix}.
$$

Then $\Pi_0=\langle\lambda_0,\lambda_1\rangle$ is free
of rank two, normal of indices 12 and 24 in SL and GL.
For $S=\operatorname{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)/[\Pi_0,\Pi_0]$,

$$
S=\mathbb Z^2\rtimes H,\qquad H=\operatorname{Dic}_3,
$$

where, in this presentation, $r^6=1$, $s^2=r^3$,
$srs^{-1}=r^{-1}$, and the action is

$$
R=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\1&-1\end{pmatrix},\quad
 J=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}.
$$

The marking is $\rho\mapsto(-e_0,r)$, $\sigma\mapsto(0,s)$,
$\lambda_i\mapsto(e_i,1)$. This group is virtually
abelian but not modern metabelian.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
C.2 identifies the projective level-two kernel as free
on the corresponding elementary matrices.
The signed lifts $\lambda_i$ give a complement to
$\{\pm I\}$ in $\Gamma(2)$, hence index 12.
Modulo four their subgroup is

$$
K=\left\{I,
 \begin{pmatrix}3&2\\0&3\end{pmatrix},
 \begin{pmatrix}3&0\\2&3\end{pmatrix},
 \begin{pmatrix}1&2\\2&1\end{pmatrix}\right\}.
$$

Conjugation by $\rho$ sends its generators $a,b$ to
$b,ab$, and $\sigma$ exchanges them.
The kernel modulo two in SL modulo four has order eight
and is generated by $a,b,-I$; the total order is 48.
The inverse image of $K$ thus has index 12 and equals
$\Pi_0$. Finally $\operatorname{diag}(-1,1)$ inverts
both $\lambda_i$, proving GL normality and index 24.

The quotient has the displayed dicyclic presentation
and exactly 12 elements by the index.
The matrices satisfy $R^3=J^2=I$, $JRJ=R^{-1}$.
The marked $\rho,\sigma$ satisfy their modular relations,
and direct multiplication sends the two free basis
elements to the lattice basis. The resulting surjection
has kernel exactly $[\Pi_0,\Pi_0]$.
In the derived group, $(1,-1)$ and $r^2$ have commutator
$(3,0)\ne0$, so the group is not metabelian.
The finite quotient at two independently has derived
orders $48,12,4,1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.19}{Proved}{Coordinates, points, and fibres of S}
The finite model is
$\underline S=\mathbb G_a^2\rtimes H$ with ring
$\prod_H\mathbb Z[X,Y]$: twelve smooth affine planes
in every characteristic.
The envelope ring is

$$
\varinjlim_n
 \prod_{(a,h)\in(\mathbb Z/n)^2\rtimes H}\mathbb Z[T_1,T_2].
$$

For $m=nq$ and $b=a+nc$, transitions are $T_n=c+qT_m$.
Furthermore

$$
\widehat S=\widehat{\mathbb Z}^{\,2}\rtimes H,\quad
 \operatorname{Env}(S)(\mathbb Z)=S,\quad
 \operatorname{Env}(S)(\mathbb Z_p)=\widehat S,\quad
 \operatorname{Env}(S)(\widehat{\mathbb Z})=\prod_p\widehat S.
$$

For the finite model,
$\underline S(\widehat{\mathbb Z})=\prod_p(\mathbb Z_p^2\rtimes H)$;
its common-component subset is $\widehat S$.
The full special fibre is constant $\widehat S$, not
twelve affine planes.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
Represent the point in component $(a,h)$ as $(a+nT,h)$.
Its semidirect multiplication gives the Hopf operations
with integral carries and all transition formulas.
These finite quotients are cofinal, proving the completion.
At integral points every stage is the same group $S$,
with identity transition.
At $\mathbb Z_p$ a compatible component label is
$(a,h)\in\widehat S$, and the first lattice coordinate
is forced by $x-a_n\in n\mathbb Z_p^2$ to be $a_p$.
Conversely $(a_p-a_n)/n$ supplies all higher coordinates.
Affine functors preserve products of rings.
E.14 gives the full special fibre.
The marked generators generate quotients of orders
$12n^2$ for $n=1,2,3,4,8,9$, as actually checked.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.20}{Proved}{The specified relative quotient}
The quotient of relative unipotent completion of
$\operatorname{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)\to H$ by the closed
commutator of its prounipotent kernel is
$\underline S_{\mathbb Q}$, with E.18's marking.
It is not the full integral envelope.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
A lift into an extension of $H$ by a vector group kills
$[\Pi_0,\Pi_0]$, hence factors through $S$.
E.16 extends it uniquely to $\underline S_{\mathbb Q}$.
That group itself is such an extension, proving the
universal property. Abelianizing a prounipotent kernel
represents precisely this problem.
The full GL variant has 24 components; the envelope
also retains every lattice refinement.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.21}{Open}{The full normalizer action remains unfinished}
A sign-consistent construction of all actions on C4
scans 36--41 on one marked integral relative-completion
model is not completed here.
Proved: $\underline S_{\mathbb Q}$ has dimension two
and cannot be $M_{\mathbb Q}$ of dimension six.
B.23 computes the finer three-dimensional parameter
scheme. Inner conjugation acts on $\underline S$,
with kernel the constant $\langle r^3\rangle$
over $\mathbb Z[1/6]$.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}[Partial assertions and gap]
A centralizing $(v,h)$ must have $h$ in the kernel
$\langle r^3\rangle$ of the lattice action.
Then $v$ is fixed by $R,J$; $\det(R-I)=3$ forces
$v=0$ on the stated base.
This proves the inner action and kernel.
Dimension excludes the proposed group-scheme identification.
The finite checks in E.19 and B.23 do not identify an
external parameter group with a full automorphism
functor or verify the entire sign-bearing action.
The arithmetic quotient in E.28 is also outside its
proved scope.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.22}{Proved}{An explicit Stallings-type test}
For $F=\langle x,y\rangle$, let
$f(x)=x$, $f(y)=y[x,y]$.
It induces automorphisms of every lower-central quotient,
every pro-$\ell$ completion, and the rational Malcev
completion. It is not onto $F$ or its full profinite
completion. The representation $x\mapsto(12)$,
$y\mapsto(13)$ has image six and, after $f$, image two.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
The map is identity on abelianization.
Each graded lower-central piece is generated by
commutators in the generators, so changing a generator
by a weight-two term changes no associated graded term.
Induction on the central series gives an automorphism
of each lower-central quotient.

On the free pro-$\ell$ group it is identity modulo
$\Phi(P)=\overline{P^\ell[P,P]}$.
Its compact image is closed. A proper image would
remain proper in a finite $\ell$-quotient and lie
in a maximal subgroup of index $\ell$, contradicting
Frattini surjectivity.
A finitely generated profinite group is Hopfian:
there are finitely many open normal subgroups of any
given index, and inverse image under a surjective
endomorphism permutes them. Some iterate preserves each,
forcing the kernel into every open normal subgroup.
Thus the map is an automorphism.

On the completed free Lie algebra it sends each generator
to itself plus higher degree. A continuous inverse is
constructed recursively degree by degree.
Finally direct multiplication of the two transpositions
gives $y[x,y]=x$. The image drops from six to two,
which is impossible for a surjective endomorphism.
The finite-group program checks this.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.23}{Refuted}{The two S3-cover claims differ}
The representation of $\Pi_0$ sending its free basis
to $(12),(13)$ cannot factor through $S$ or its marked
envelope. The modern metabelian quotient
$\Pi_0/\Pi_0''$ retains it but is not virtually abelian.
In contrast, ordinary reduction
$\operatorname{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)\to S_3$ factors through
$S$ and its envelope.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
The first images do not commute, while $S$ kills
$[\Pi_0,\Pi_0]$. Since $S_3''=1$, the modern metabelian
quotient retains them.
For a free rank-two group, the universal abelian
covering graph has boundary

$$
\mathbb Z[u^{\pm1},v^{\pm1}]^2
 \xrightarrow{(u-1,v-1)}\mathbb Z[u^{\pm1},v^{\pm1}].
$$

Its kernel is freely generated over the Laurent ring
by $(1-v,u-1)$: divisibility by the prime $u-1$
proves this directly. There are no two-cells.
Thus $F'/F''$ is the additive Laurent polynomial group,
not finitely generated. Every subgroup of a finitely
generated virtually abelian group is finitely generated,
so $F/F''$ is not virtually abelian.

Reduction modulo two kills $\Pi_0$ and factors through
$H/\langle r^3\rangle=S_3$, a retained finite quotient.
Its restriction to $\Pi_0$ is trivial and is therefore
a different representation.
In E.22 the transitive three-letter action pulls back
to a two-element orbit and a fixed point, giving a
disconnected pulled-back degree-three cover.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.24}{Known}{The two rational theories}
Fibrewise rationalization preserves the discrete
fundamental group of $BS=K(S,1)$ and changes no higher
homotopy groups, so $BS$ is already fibrewise rational.
Its rational local-system derived category is $D(\mathbb Q[S])$.
The fundamental affine group of its schematization is
$S_{\mathbb Q}^{\rm alg}$, whereas

$$
\operatorname{Env}(S)_{\mathbb Q}
 =(\mathbb G_a^2\times\widehat{\mathbb Z}_{\rm const}^{\,2})
       \rtimes H.
$$

All retain the reduction quotient to $S_3$.
The marked maps of $S$ into its envelope and full
proalgebraic completion are injective.
No assertion about the entire schematization being a
classifying stack is made without a goodness hypothesis.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}[Exact reductions and explicit calculation]
The fundamental-group-preserving rational equivalences
are those of \cite[Theorem 3]{E_RiveraWierstraZeinalian2021}.
There are no higher groups to rationalize in $BS$.
Descent from its contractible universal cover identifies
complexes of local systems with derived $\mathbb Q[S]$-modules.
The schematic fundamental-group statement is
\cite[Corollary 3.3.7]{E_Toen2006}.

At a rational envelope stage, use $X=a+nT$.
The multiplication becomes
$(X,a,h)(Y,b,k)=(X+hY,a+hb,hk)$.
Taking the inverse limit gives the displayed formula
and marking $(v,h)\mapsto((v,\widehat v),h)$.
The finite quotients $(\mathbb Z/n)^2\rtimes H$ separate
all elements; their permutation representations also
separate elements in full proalgebraic completion.
The finite quotient $S\to H\to S_3$ survives throughout.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.25}{Proved}{Representations separating the theories}
A twelve-dimensional rational representation of $S$
extends to full proalgebraic completion but not its
envelope. The regular local system $\mathbb Q[S]$
is not the restriction of any rational comodule of
an affine group scheme along a marked map from $S$.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
Induce $\chi(a,b)=2^a$ from $L=\mathbb Z^2$ to $S=L\rtimes H$.
On basis $e_h$, let $v e_h=\chi(h^{-1}v)e_h$ and let
$H$ permute the basis. The lattice generator has
eigenvalue two. An envelope representation factors
through a finite stage, where a positive power of
each marked element lies on an additive subgroup;
its eigenvalues must be roots of unity.
Thus this representation does not extend to the envelope,
but extends by the definition of full proalgebraic completion.

Every vector in a rational comodule lies in a finite-dimensional
subcomodule. Indeed write
$\delta(v)=\sum_i v_i\otimes a_i$ with independent $a_i$.
The counit puts $v$ in $W=\operatorname{span}(v_i)$.
Projecting the first tensor factor of coassociativity
to $V/W$ gives $\delta(v_i)\in W\otimes B$.
But the orbit of $1$ in $\mathbb Q[S]$ contains the
whole infinite basis $S$, so cannot lie in such a
finite-dimensional invariant space.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.26}{Proved}{The nilpotent completions of S}
For $L_0=(R-I)\mathbb Z^2$,

$$
S'=L_0\rtimes\langle r^2\rangle,\qquad
 S^{ab}=C_{12},\qquad \gamma_nS=S'\ (n\ge2).
$$

Its lower-central and finite pronilpotent completions
are $C_{12}$; its pro-two and pro-three completions
are $C_4,C_3$; other pro-$\ell$ completions and its
characteristic-zero Malcev completion are trivial.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
The columns of $J-I$ lie in $(R-I)\mathbb Z^2$,
whose index is three.
Also $H'=\langle r^2\rangle=C_3$ and $H^{ab}=C_4$.
This computes $S'$ and $S^{ab}=C_3\times C_4$.
Since $R^2-I=(R-I)(R+I)$ and $\det(R+I)=1$,
$[L,\langle r^2\rangle]=L_0$.
Also $[s,r^2]=r^2$, so $[S,S']=S'$.
Every nilpotent quotient therefore factors through
$C_{12}$. Taking Sylow quotients gives the pro-$\ell$
claims. A characteristic-zero unipotent group is nilpotent
and torsion-free, by logarithm, so all such images vanish.
The finite quotients are envelope quotients.
The checked mod-two quotient has lower-central orders
$48,12,12,12$ and derived orders $48,12,4,1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.27}{Open}{Two exact general-base residuals}
Unproved here are:
existence for a nonlinear separated locally finitely
presented group over an arbitrary $\mathbb Q$-algebra
with a geometrically additive section;
and the arbitrary-noetherian-base envelope universal
property for affine targets without a supplied linear
embedding. Specific unresolved instances in this work
are a nonlinear group over $\mathbb Q[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^2)$
and such an affine group over $\mathbb Z[t]$.
These are not claims of literature-wide openness.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}[Proved range and missing inputs]
E.1 proves all closed linear cases over $\mathbb Q$-algebras
and general groups over characteristic-zero fields.
Nilpotents alone are not an obstruction in that range:

$$
g=\begin{pmatrix}1+\epsilon&1\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 u(T)=\begin{pmatrix}
 1+\epsilon T&T+\epsilon(T^2-T)/2\\0&1
 \end{pmatrix}
$$

satisfies $u(T+U)=u(T)u(U)$ and $u(1)=g$.
The program checks these identities modulo $\epsilon^2$.
Without a linear embedding, the missing input is
existence of the interpolating morphism into the
nonlinear target; fieldwise descent alone does not
construct a map on a thickening.
For the general noetherian affine case the missing
input is an appropriate positive-power theorem.
Once additive lattice maps exist, E.13 handles their
finite torsion defects and uniqueness after refinement.
\end{proof}

\begin{statement}{E.28}{Proved}{Discrete Fermat relative completions}
Let $F=\pi_1(\mathbb P^1(\mathbb C)\setminus\{0,1,\infty\})
=\langle x,y\rangle$ and
$q_n:F\to A_n=(\mathbb Z/n)^2$ send meridians to its basis.
Relative completion over $\mathbb Q$ has free prounipotent
kernel of rank $n^2+1$.
Its abelian-kernel quotient has dimension $n^2+1$,
equal to five at $n=2$; its full completion is not
of finite type. It cannot be $M_{\mathbb Q}$.
For $n\ge2$ its abelian-kernel quotient cannot be
$\underline S_{\mathbb Q}$.
The arithmetic/profinite comparison relative to
$S_3\times\widehat{\mathbb Z}^{\times}$ remains unresolved.
\end{statement}
\begin{proof}
The covering graph of the two-petal rose has $n^2$
vertices and $2n^2$ positive edges.
Contracting a spanning tree leaves $n^2+1$ edges,
so $K_n=\ker q_n$ is free of that rank.
This is the connected cover adjoining $n$th roots
of $z$ and $1-z$.
The executed graph check covers $1\le n\le12$.
At two it gives the basis

$$
x^2,\quad yxy^{-1}x^{-1},\quad y^2,\quad
 xyxy^{-1},\quad xy^2x^{-1}.
$$


Let $U_n$ be exponential of the completed free Lie
algebra on that basis.
Assigning logarithms of the generators uniquely
extends a map from $K_n$ to any finite-dimensional
unipotent group, and then to inverse limits.
Thus $U_n$ is its unipotent completion.
Choose representatives $s(a)$ in $F$.
Their conjugations extend to $C_a$ on $U_n$, and put
$c(a,b)=s(a)s(b)s(a+b)^{-1}$ in $U_n$.
They satisfy

$$
C_aC_b=\operatorname{Inn}(c(a,b))C_{a+b},\qquad
 c(a,b)c(a+b,d)=C_a(c(b,d))c(a,b+d).
$$

Hence

$$
(u,a)(v,b)=(uC_a(v)c(a,b),a+b)
$$

defines a proalgebraic extension with the given marking.
Any competing relative extension receives the unique
map from $U_n$; uniqueness forces compatibility with
the conjugations and cocycle, extending it on each
component. This is the relative universal property.

For $s'(a)=b_as(a)$,

$$
C'_a=\operatorname{Inn}(b_a)C_a,\qquad
 c'(a,b)=b_aC_a(b_b)c(a,b)b_{a+b}^{-1}.
$$

The isomorphism to old coordinates is $(u,a)\mapsto(ub_a,a)$,
as direct substitution shows.
Thus there is no dependence on the section.

The free Lie abelianization has dimension $n^2+1$.
For at least two generators, $\operatorname{ad}(X)^mY$
has associative word $X^mY$ with coefficient one,
so nonzero degrees occur arbitrarily far out.
The full kernel is not of finite type.
No square $n^2$ equals five, so the abelian-kernel
dimension is never six, and exceeds two for $n\ge2$.
These are discrete complex comparisons; adjoining
the requested arithmetic quotient requires its own
marking, topology and representation category.
\end{proof}
The paper source is now delivered through E.28, together with the low-type and orientation qualifications in F. The following blocks supply the executable replacement calculations and their complete outputs. The 
Z
/
4
Z/4 presentation search was rerun in the surviving JavaScript runtime: it checked all 2,880 words in the stated family and found 64 descended words, with four square-determinant images.

code/c_replacement.js
function verifyC(){
const mod=(a,n)=>((a%n)+n)%n;
function tc(signed,n){
const rel=[Array(signed?4:2).fill(0),Array(signed?6:3).fill(2)];
if(signed)rel.push([0,0,3,3,3]);rel.push(Array.from({length:n},()=>[0,2]).flat());
const parent=[0],edges=[];let tab=[[-1,-1,-1,-1]],version=0;
const find=x=>{while(parent[x]!==x){parent[x]=parent[parent[x]];x=parent[x];}return x;};
function join(a,b){a=find(a);b=find(b);if(a===b)return false;parent[Math.max(a,b)]=Math.min(a,b);version++;return true;}
function rebuild(){for(;;){const t=parent.map(()=>[-1,-1,-1,-1]);let changed=false;for(const[x,g,y]of edges){const a=find(x),b=find(y);if(t[a][g]<0)t[a][g]=b;else if(join(t[a][g],b))changed=true;}if(!changed){tab=t;return;}}}
function edge(a,g,b){edges.push([a,g,b],[b,g^1,a]);version++;rebuild();}
function scan(c,w){let a=find(c),b=a,i=0,j=w.length-1;while(i<=j&&tab[find(a)][w[i]]>=0)a=find(tab[find(a)][w[i++]]);while(j>=i&&tab[find(b)][w[j]^1]>=0)b=find(tab[find(b)][w[j--]^1]);if(i>j){if(join(a,b))rebuild();}else if(i===j)edge(a,w[i],b);}
for(;;){let old;do{old=version;for(let c=0;c<parent.length;c++)if(find(c)===c)for(const w of rel)scan(c,w);}while(version!==old);
let gap=null;for(let c=0;c<parent.length&&!gap;c++)if(find(c)===c)for(let g=0;g<4;g++)if(tab[c][g]<0){gap=[c,g];break;}
if(!gap)break;if(parent.length>5000)throw Error("Coset cap; no conclusion");const d=parent.length;parent.push(d);tab.push([-1,-1,-1,-1]);edge(gap[0],gap[1],d);}
const reps=parent.map((_,i)=>i).filter(i=>find(i)===i),ix=new Map(reps.map((x,i)=>[x,i]));
const perms=[0,2].map(g=>reps.map(c=>ix.get(find(tab[c][g]))));let verified=true;
for(const c of reps)for(const w of rel){let d=c;for(const g of w)d=find(tab[d][g]);if(d!==c)verified=false;}
return{kind:signed?"signed":"projective",n,order:reps.length,allocated:parent.length,relators_verified:verified,s:perms[0],r:perms[1]};}
function ring(q,kind="residue"){
const add=kind==="F4"?(a,b)=>a^b:(a,b)=>mod(a+b,q),neg=kind==="F4"?a=>a:a=>mod(-a,q);
const mul=kind==="F4"?(a,b)=>{let x=0;for(let j=0;j<2;j++)if((b>>j)&1)x^=a<<j;if(x&4)x^=7;return x;}:(a,b)=>mod(a*b,q);
const units=Array.from({length:q},(_,a)=>a).filter(a=>Array.from({length:q},(_,b)=>b).some(b=>mul(a,b)===1));return{q,add,neg,mul,units};}
function matrixOps(R){
const{q,add,neg,mul,units}=R,key=A=>A.reduce((v,a,j)=>v+a*q**j,0);
const times=(A,B)=>[add(mul(A[0],B[0]),mul(A[1],B[2])),add(mul(A[0],B[1]),mul(A[1],B[3])),add(mul(A[2],B[0]),mul(A[3],B[2])),add(mul(A[2],B[1]),mul(A[3],B[3]))];
const det=A=>add(mul(A[0],A[3]),neg(mul(A[1],A[2]))),canon=A=>units.map(u=>A.map(a=>mul(u,a))).sort((A,B)=>key(A)-key(B))[0];
const enumerate=()=>{let all=[];for(let a=0;a<q;a++)for(let b=0;b<q;b++)for(let c=0;c<q;c++)for(let d=0;d<q;d++)all.push([a,b,c,d]);return all;};
function generated(gs,projective=false){const I=[1,0,0,1],out=[I],seen=new Set([key(I)]);for(let i=0;i<out.length;i++)for(const g of gs){let A=times(out[i],g);if(projective)A=canon(A);const k=key(A);if(!seen.has(k)){seen.add(k);out.push(A);}}return out;}
return{key,times,det,canon,enumerate,generated};}
const cosets=[];for(const signed of[false,true])for(const n of[2,3,4,5])cosets.push(tc(signed,n));
const counts=[],missed={};
for(const[name,q,kind]of[["F2",2],["F3",3],["F4",4,"F4"],["F5",5],["F7",7],["Z4",4],["Z9",9]]){
const R=ring(q,kind),O=matrixOps(R),all=O.enumerate(),sl=all.filter(A=>O.det(A)===1),gl=all.filter(A=>R.units.includes(O.det(A)));
const pgl=new Map(gl.map(A=>{const B=O.canon(A);return[O.key(B),B];})),psl=new Set(sl.map(A=>O.key(O.canon(A))));
const gs=[[0,R.neg(1),1,0],...Array.from({length:q},(_,t)=>[1,R.neg(t),0,1])],gen=O.generated(gs),missing=[...pgl.keys()].filter(k=>!psl.has(k)).sort((a,b)=>a-b);
counts.push({ring:name,SL2:sl.length,GL2:gl.length,PSL_word_image:psl.size,PGL2:pgl.size,elementary_image:gen.length,missed:missing.length});
missed[name]={base:q,encoding:"a+q*b+q^2*c+q^3*d; least scalar representative",codes:missing};}
const split=t=>[t%4,Math.floor(t/4)];
const add16=(a,b)=>{a=split(a);b=split(b);return mod(a[0]+b[0],4)+4*mod(a[1]+b[1],4);};
const neg16=a=>{a=split(a);return mod(-a[0],4)+4*mod(-a[1],4);};
const mul16=(a,b)=>{a=split(a);b=split(b);return mod(a[0]*b[0]-a[1]*b[1],4)+4*mod(a[0]*b[1]+a[1]*b[0]-a[1]*b[1],4);};
const tau=a=>{a=split(a);return mod(a[0]-a[1],4)+4*mod(-a[1],4);};
const R16={q:16,add:add16,neg:neg16,mul:mul16,units:Array.from({length:16},(_,a)=>a).filter(a=>Array.from({length:16},(_,b)=>b).some(b=>mul16(a,b)===1))};
const R4=ring(4),O4=matrixOps(R4),O16=matrixOps(R16),U4=a=>[1,mod(-a,4),0,1],U16=a=>[1,neg16(a),0,1];
const H=O4.generated([[0,3,1,0],U4(1)],true),C=new Set([0,1,2,3].map(a=>O4.key(O4.canon(U4(a)))));
const hs=H.filter(h=>!C.has(O4.key(h))),as=Array.from({length:12},(_,i)=>i+4),target=new Map(H.map(h=>[O16.key(O16.canon(h)),O4.key(h)]));
const records=[],image=new Set();let checked=0;
for(const a of as)for(const h of hs)for(const b of as){checked++;const ca=add16(tau(a),neg16(a)),cb=add16(tau(b),neg16(b));if(ca>=4||cb>=4)continue;const hh=O4.canon(O4.times(O4.times(U4(ca),h),U4(cb)));if(O4.key(hh)!==O4.key(h))continue;
const A=O16.canon(O16.times(O16.times(U16(a),h),U16(b)));if(!target.has(O16.key(A)))throw Error("Descended target not in listed PSL2(Z4)");records.push([a,O4.key(h),b]);image.add(target.get(O16.key(A)));}
const aset=[...new Set(records.map(x=>x[0]))].sort((a,b)=>a-b),hset=[...new Set(records.map(x=>x[1]))].sort((a,b)=>a-b),bset=[...new Set(records.map(x=>x[2]))].sort((a,b)=>a-b);
const descent={ring:"Z4[x]/(x^2+x+1)",tested:checked,fixed_words:records.length,a_codes:aset,h_matrix_codes:hset,b_codes:bset,cartesian_product_complete:records.length===aset.length*hset.length*bset.length,target_PSL_codes:[...image].sort((a,b)=>a-b),all_targets_square_determinant:true};
return{cosets,counts,missed,descent,limits:["Coset enumeration concerns abstract word pushouts, not fppf sheaf points.","Z4 descent search covers only U(a)hU(b) over the specified quadratic étale ring.","The Z4 full projective sheaf image remains 24 or 48."]};}
console.log(JSON.stringify(verifyC()));

const result = (function(){
const out=[], emit=x=>out.push(x);
const gcd=(a,b)=>b?gcd(b,a%b):a, lcm=(a,b)=>a/gcd(a,b)*b;
const mod=(a,n)=>(a%n+n)%n;
const I=[1,0,0,1], key=a=>Array.isArray(a)?a.join(","):String(a);
function matmul(a,b,n){return [mod(a[0]*b[0]+a[1]*b[2],n),mod(a[0]*b[1]+a[1]*b[3],n),mod(a[2]*b[0]+a[3]*b[2],n),mod(a[2]*b[1]+a[3]*b[3],n)];}
function matinv(a,n){return [mod(a[3],n),mod(-a[1],n),mod(-a[2],n),mod(a[0],n)];}
function pow(a,k,mul,one){let z=one;if(k<0)throw Error("negative exponent");while(k){if(k&1)z=mul(z,a);a=mul(a,a);k=Math.floor(k/2);}return z;}
function closure(gens,mul,one){const seen=new Map([[key(one),one]]), queue=[one];for(let j=0;j<queue.length;j++)for(const g of gens){const h=mul(queue[j],g),k=key(h);if(!seen.has(k)){seen.set(k,h);queue.push(h);}}return queue;}
function order(a,mul,one){let b=one;for(let k=1;k<10000;k++){b=mul(b,a);if(key(b)===key(one))return k;}throw Error("order cap");}
function mapCheck(H,X,Y,AX,AY,mul,inv,one){
 const im=new Map([[key(one),one]]), queue=[one];
 const gens=[X,Y,inv(X),inv(Y)], imgs=[AX,AY,inv(AX),inv(AY)];
 for(let j=0;j<queue.length;j++)for(let a=0;a<4;a++){
  const h=mul(queue[j],gens[a]), v=mul(im.get(key(queue[j])),imgs[a]), k=key(h);
  if(im.has(k)){if(key(im.get(k))!==key(v))return false;}
  else{im.set(k,v);queue.push(h);}
 }
 return im.size===H.length && new Set([...im.values()].map(key)).size===H.length;
}
for(const n of [2,3,4,6,12]){
 const mul=(a,b)=>matmul(a,b,n), inv=a=>matinv(a,n), one=I.map(x=>mod(x,n));
 const X=[-1,2,0,-1].map(x=>mod(x,n)), Y=[-1,0,-2,-1].map(x=>mod(x,n));
 const Z=inv(mul(X,Y)), S=[0,-1,1,0].map(x=>mod(x,n)), rho=[0,1,-1,1].map(x=>mod(x,n));
 const R=mul(inv(Y),rho), R2=mul(R,R), conj=(a,b)=>mul(mul(a,b),inv(a));
 const H=closure([X,Y],mul,one);
 const comm=[];for(const a of H)for(const b of H)comm.push(mul(mul(mul(a,b),inv(a)),inv(b)));
 const derived=closure([...new Map(comm.map(x=>[key(x),x])).values()],mul,one);
 const exponent=H.reduce((e,a)=>lcm(e,order(a,mul,one)),1);
 let count=0, automorphisms=0;
 for(let lambda=0;lambda<2*exponent;lambda++)if(gcd(lambda,2*exponent)===1){
  const m=(lambda-1)/2;
  for(const F of derived){
   const dual=mul(conj(S,F),F);
   const hex=[conj(R2,F),pow(Z,m,mul,one),conj(R,F),pow(Y,m,mul,one),F,pow(X,m,mul,one)].reduce(mul,one);
   if(key(dual)!==key(one)||key(hex)!==key(one))continue;
   count++;
   const AX=pow(X,lambda,mul,one), AY=mul(mul(inv(F),pow(Y,lambda,mul,one)),F);
   if(mapCheck(H,X,Y,AX,AY,mul,inv,one))automorphisms++;
  }
 }
 emit({test:"finite_GT_shadows",n,H:H.length,derived:derived.length,exponent,pairs:count,automorphisms});
}
let cocycles=0;const V=[0,1,2,3], nonzeroPairs=[];
for(const a of [1,2,3])for(const b of [1,2,3])nonzeroPairs.push([a,b]);
const cocycleKeys=new Set(), coboundaryKeys=new Set();
for(let bits=0;bits<512;bits++){
 const f=(a,b)=>{if(!a||!b)return 0;return (bits>>nonzeroPairs.findIndex(p=>p[0]===a&&p[1]===b&&p[1]===b))&1;};
 if(V.every(a=>V.every(b=>V.every(c=>(f(a,b)^f(a^b,c)^f(b,c)^f(a,b^c))===0)))){
  cocycles++;cocycleKeys.add(bits);
 }
}
for(let bits=0;bits<8;bits++){
 const f=a=>a?((bits>>(a-1))&1):0;
 let v=0;nonzeroPairs.forEach(([a,b],i)=>{v|=(f(a)^f(b)^f(a^b))<<i;});coboundaryKeys.add(v);
}
emit({test:"H2_C2_squared",normalized_two_cochains:512,cocycles,coboundaries:coboundaryKeys.size,classes:cocycles/coboundaryKeys.size});
const classification=[];
for(let a=0;a<2;a++)for(let b=0;b<2;b++)for(let c=0;c<2;c++){
 const q=[a,b,a^b^c];
 classification.push({a,b,c,squares_on_10_01_11:q,group:c?(a&&b?"Q8":"D8"):(a||b?"C4xC2":"C2^3")});
}
emit({test:"central_extension_table",rows:classification});
const f8mul=(a,b)=>{let c=0;while(b){if(b&1)c^=a;b>>=1;a<<=1;if(a&8)a^=11;}return c;};
const f8mm=(a,b)=>[f8mul(a[0],b[0])^f8mul(a[1],b[2]),f8mul(a[0],b[1])^f8mul(a[1],b[3]),f8mul(a[2],b[0])^f8mul(a[3],b[2]),f8mul(a[2],b[1])^f8mul(a[3],b[3])];
const f8inv=a=>[a[3],a[1],a[2],a[0]];
const A=[0,1,1,0], B=[0,2,5,1], G=closure([A,B],f8mm,I);
const left=new Map(G.map(x=>[key(x),x])), classes=[];
while(left.size){
 const g=left.values().next().value;
 const cls=[...new Map(G.map(h=>{const x=f8mm(f8mm(h,g),f8inv(h));return [key(x),x];})).values()];
 cls.forEach(h=>left.delete(key(h)));
 classes.push({order:order(g,f8mm,I),size:cls.length,normal_closure:closure(cls,f8mm,I).length});
}
classes.sort((a,b)=>a.order-b.order||a.size-b.size);
emit({test:"SL2_F8",field_polynomial:"x^3+x+1",alpha:2,alpha_inverse:5,A,B,A_order:order(A,f8mm,I),B_order:order(B,f8mm,I),AB_order:order(f8mm(A,B),f8mm,I),generated_order:G.length,classes});
const torsion=[];
for(let a=0;a<5;a++)for(let b=0;b<5;b++){
 const x=[a,b,mod(-b,5),mod(a+b,5)], norm=mod(a*a+a*b+b*b,5);
 if(norm && key(pow(x,4,(a,b)=>matmul(a,b,5),I))===key(I))torsion.push({a,b,norm});
}
emit({test:"four_torsion_norm_mod5",elements:torsion,norms:[...new Set(torsion.map(x=>x.norm))].sort()});
const hensel=[];let root=2,modulus=5;
for(let k=1;k<=6;k++){
 hensel.push({k,modulus,root,residual:mod(root*root+1,modulus)});
 if(k<6){let next=null;for(let j=0;j<5;j++){const r=root+j*modulus;if(mod(r*r+1,modulus*5)===0){next=r;break;}}root=next;modulus*=5;}
}
emit({test:"sqrt_minus1_at5",lifts:hensel});
emit({test:"rank_obstruction_at7",rows:[2,3,4].map(r=>({r,ambient_principal_units:7**(2*(r-1)),image_upper_bound:7**(r-1),index_lower_bound:7**(r-1)}))});
const sq4=new Set();
for(let a=0;a<4;a++)for(let b=0;b<4;b++)if((a%2)||(b%2)){
 sq4.add(key([mod(a*a-b*b,4),mod(2*a*b+b*b,4)]));
}
emit({test:"dyadic_unit_squares_mod4",squares:[...sq4].sort(),minus1:"3,0",sqrt_minus3:"3,2",minus_sqrt_minus3:"1,2"});
let points=1;for(let x=0;x<5;x++)for(let y=0;y<5;y++)if(mod(y*y-x*x*x+x,5)===0)points++;
emit({test:"elliptic_E_y2_x3_minus_x",p:5,points,trace:6-points,frobenius_polynomial:[1,points-6,5]});
const rho=[0,1,-1,1],sigma=[0,-1,1,0], P=[1,1,0,1], Pi=[1,-1,0,1];
const intmm=(a,b)=>[a[0]*b[0]+a[1]*b[2],a[0]*b[1]+a[1]*b[3],a[2]*b[0]+a[3]*b[2],a[2]*b[1]+a[3]*b[3]];
const rr=intmm(intmm(P,rho),Pi), cusp=intmm(sigma,rr);
emit({test:"nonmotivic_cusp",rho_image:rr,sigma_image:sigma,cusp,trace:cusp[0]+cusp[3],determinant:cusp[0]*cusp[3]-cusp[1]*cusp[2],characteristic_polynomial:[1,-4,1]});
const roots=[];for(let x=-1;x<=1;x+=2)roots.push({x,value:x*x*x-x*x-3*x+1});
const cubic=[1,-1,-3,1], [aa,bb,cc,dd]=cubic, disc=bb*bb*cc*cc-4*aa*cc**3-4*bb**3*dd-27*aa*aa*dd*dd+18*aa*bb*cc*dd;
const H37=[];for(let j=0;j<9;j++)H37.push(pow(16,j,(a,b)=>a*b%37,1));
const cosets=[];for(let j=0;j<4;j++)cosets.push(H37.map(h=>h*pow(2,j,(a,b)=>a*b%37,1)%37).sort((a,b)=>a-b));
const conv=(a,b)=>{const z=Array(37).fill(0);for(let i=0;i<37;i++)for(let j=0;j<37;j++)z[(i+j)%37]+=a[i]*b[j];return z;};
const cycOne=Array(37).fill(0);cycOne[0]=1;
let poly=[cycOne];
for(const coset of cosets){
 const eta=Array(37).fill(0);coset.forEach(i=>eta[i]=1);
 const next=Array.from({length:poly.length+1},()=>Array(37).fill(0));
 poly.forEach((p,i)=>{const q=conv(p,eta);for(let j=0;j<37;j++){next[i][j]-=q[j];next[i+1][j]+=p[j];}});
 poly=next;
}
const coefficients=poly.map(p=>{if(!p.slice(1).every(x=>x===p[1]))throw Error("non-rational coefficient");return p[0]-p[1];});
emit({test:"Dic3_CFT_example",cubic,disc,rational_root_tests:roots,primitive_root2_order:order(2,(a,b)=>a*b%37,1),period_cosets:cosets,quartic_coefficients_descending:coefficients.slice().reverse()});
const phi=n=>{let c=0;for(let a=0;a<n;a++)if(gcd(a,n)===1)c++;return c;};
const generation=[];
for(let n=1;n<=48;n++){
 if(n===1){generation.push({n,SL2_order:1,generated_SL2:1,GL2_index:1});continue;}
 const one=I.map(x=>mod(x,n)),mul=(a,b)=>matmul(a,b,n);
 const gens=[rho.map(x=>mod(x,n)),sigma.map(x=>mod(x,n))];
 const gr=closure(gens,mul,one).length;
 let expected=n**3, q=n;for(let p=2;p<=q;p++)if(q%p===0){expected=expected/p/p*(p*p-1);while(q%p===0)q/=p;}
 if(gr!==expected)throw Error("generation mismatch "+n);
 generation.push({n,SL2_order:gr,generated_SL2:gr,GL2_index:phi(n)/(n>2?2:1)});
}
emit({test:"modular_generation_n_1_to_48",rows:generation});
emit({test:"integral_cover_classes",characters:["1","delta","e","t","delta*e","delta*t","e*t","delta*e*t"],count:8,F5_point:{a:1,b:2,c:1,d:1,e:1,t:1,delta:2},F5_squares:[0,1,4]});
const dicOrders=[];
for(let a=0;a<3;a++)for(const b of [1,3]){
 let x=0,y=0,k=0;
 do{x=mod(x+(y%2?-a:a),3);y=mod(y+b,4);k++;}while(x||y);
 dicOrders.push({a,b,order:k});
}
emit({test:"Dic3_imaginary_obstruction",outside_index2_orders:dicOrders});
const roots37=[];
for(let x=0;x<37;x++)if(mod(x*x*x-x*x-3*x+1,37)===0)
 roots37.push({x,multiple:mod(3*x*x-2*x-3,37)===0});
emit({test:"cubic_reduction_at37",roots:roots37,Eisenstein_shift_coefficients:[1,2,-2,-2]});
return out;
})();

"use strict";
function verifyEArithmetic() {
  const check=(c,m)=>{if(!c)throw Error(m);};
  const abs=n=>n<0n?-n:n;
  const gcd=(a,b)=>{a=abs(a);b=abs(b);while(b){const c=a%b;a=b;b=c;}return a;};
  const Q=(a,b=1n)=>{a=BigInt(a);b=BigInt(b);check(b!==0n,"zero denominator");if(b<0n){a=-a;b=-b;}const d=gcd(a,b);return [a/d,b/d];};
  const qa=(a,b)=>Q(a[0]*b[1]+b[0]*a[1],a[1]*b[1]);
  const qn=a=>[-a[0],a[1]];
  const qs=(a,b)=>qa(a,qn(b));
  const qm=(a,b)=>Q(a[0]*b[0],a[1]*b[1]);
  const qd=(a,b)=>Q(a[0]*b[1],a[1]*b[0]);
  const qe=(a,b)=>a[0]===b[0]&&a[1]===b[1];
  const qstr=a=>a[1]===1n?String(a[0]):String(a[0])+"/"+String(a[1]);
  const fact=n=>{let f=1n;for(let i=2;i<=n;i++)f*=BigInt(i);return f;};
  const zero=n=>Array.from({length:n},()=>Array.from({length:n},()=>Q(0)));
  const eye=n=>{const A=zero(n);for(let i=0;i<n;i++)A[i][i]=Q(1);return A;};
  const ma=(A,B)=>A.map((r,i)=>r.map((a,j)=>qa(a,B[i][j])));
  const ms=(A,c)=>A.map(r=>r.map(a=>qm(a,c)));
  const mm=(A,B)=>A.map((r,i)=>B[0].map((_,j)=>r.reduce((s,a,k)=>qa(s,qm(a,B[k][j])),Q(0))));
  const mp=(A,n)=>{let B=eye(A.length);for(let j=0;j<n;j++)B=mm(B,A);return B;};
  const me=(A,B)=>A.every((r,i)=>r.every((a,j)=>qe(a,B[i][j])));
  const integral=A=>A.every(r=>r.every(a=>a[1]===1n));
  const mstr=A=>A.map(r=>r.map(qstr));
  const exp=A=>{let B=eye(A.length);for(let j=1;j<A.length;j++)B=ma(B,ms(mp(A,j),Q(1,fact(j))));check(me(mp(A,A.length),zero(A.length)),"nilpotence for exp");return B;};
  const log1=N=>{let A=zero(N.length);for(let j=1;j<N.length;j++)A=ma(A,ms(mp(N,j),Q(j%2?1:-1,j)));return A;};
  const X=zero(4);X[0][1]=Q(3,2);X[0][2]=Q(-2);X[1][2]=Q(4);X[2][3]=Q(1,2);
  const A=ms(mp(X,2),Q(1,2)),B=ms(mp(X,3),Q(1,6));
  check(integral(ma(A,B))&&integral(ma(A,ms(B,Q(-1)))),"signed integral combinations");
  check(!integral(A)&&!integral(B)&&!integral(exp(X)),"signed example scope");
  const twice=ms(X,Q(2));
  let content=0n;for(const r of twice)for(const a of r){check(a[1]===1n,"2X integral");content=gcd(content,a[0]);}
  check(content===1n,"2X primitive");
  const signed={X:mstr(X),X2_over_2:mstr(A),X3_over_6:mstr(B),
    plus_integral:integral(ma(A,B)),minus_integral:integral(ma(A,ms(B,Q(-1)))),
    plus_with_X3_over_2_integral:integral(ma(A,ms(mp(X,3),Q(1,2)))),
    minus_with_X3_over_2_integral:integral(ma(A,ms(mp(X,3),Q(-1,2)))),
    exp_X_integral:integral(exp(X)),content_2X:String(content)};
  let witnesses=[];
  for(let a=1;a<=7;a++)for(let b=1;b<=12;b++)for(let c=1;c<=7;c++)for(let d=-4;d<=4;d++){
    if(a*b%4===0&&b*c%4===0&&(6*d*c+a*b*c)%24===0&&(6*d*c-a*b*c)%24===0&&(d*c%4!==0||a*b*c%24!==0))
      witnesses.push([Math.max(a,2*b,c,2*Math.abs(d)),a,b,c,d]);
  }
  witnesses.sort((a,b)=>{for(let i=0;i<a.length;i++)if(a[i]!==b[i])return a[i]-b[i];return 0;});
  check(witnesses.length===46,"signed-family count");
  const J=zero(3);J[0][1]=J[1][2]=Q(1);
  const X3=log1(J);
  check(me(exp(X3),ma(eye(3),J)),"rank-three exponential");
  check(integral(exp(X3))&&integral(exp(ms(X3,Q(-1)))),"rank-three exponentials integral");
  const N=zero(5);[1,6,1,6].forEach((w,i)=>N[i][i+1]=Q(w));
  const X5=log1(N),dp5=Array.from({length:5},(_,j)=>integral(ms(mp(X5,j),Q(1,fact(j)))));
  check(integral(X5)&&me(exp(X5),ma(eye(5),N)),"rank-five integral logarithm");
  check(JSON.stringify(dp5)==="[true,true,false,true,false]","rank-five divided powers");
  const divisible=(M,p)=>M.every(r=>r.every(a=>a[1]===1n&&a[0]%BigInt(p)===0n));
  check(divisible(mp(N,2),2)&&divisible(mp(N,3),3)&&me(mp(N,5),zero(5)),"geometric-fibre tests");
  const rank_examples={rank3_log:mstr(X3),rank3_exp_integral:true,rank3_inverse_exp_integral:true,
    rank3_divided_power_flags:Array.from({length:3},(_,j)=>integral(ms(mp(X3,j),Q(1,fact(j))))),
    rank5_log:mstr(X5),rank5_divided_power_flags:dp5,rank5_top_divided_power:qstr(ms(mp(X5,4),Q(1,24))[0][4]),
    rank5_N2_divisible_by_2:true,rank5_N3_divisible_by_3:true,rank5_N5_zero:true};
  const trim=a=>{while(a.length>1&&a[a.length-1][0]===0n)a.pop();return a;};
  const pa=(a,b)=>trim(Array.from({length:Math.max(a.length,b.length)},(_,i)=>qa(a[i]||Q(0),b[i]||Q(0))));
  const ps=(a,c)=>trim(a.map(x=>qm(x,c)));
  const pm=(a,b)=>{const c=Array.from({length:a.length+b.length-1},()=>Q(0));a.forEach((x,i)=>b.forEach((y,j)=>c[i+j]=qa(c[i+j],qm(x,y))));return trim(c);};
  const pe=(a,b)=>{a=trim(a.slice());b=trim(b.slice());return a.length===b.length&&a.every((x,i)=>qe(x,b[i]));};
  const binomials=[[Q(1)]],formalExp=[[Q(1)]];
  for(let n=1;n<=12;n++){
    binomials.push(ps(pm(binomials[n-1],[Q(-(n-1)),Q(1)]),Q(1,n)));
    let sum=[Q(0)];
    for(let k=1;k<=n;k++)sum=pa(sum,ps(pm([Q(0),Q(1)],formalExp[n-k]),Q(k%2?1:-1,n)));
    formalExp.push(sum);check(pe(binomials[n],formalExp[n]),"independent exp/binomial recurrence degree "+n);
  }
  const vp=(n,p)=>{n=abs(n);check(n!==0n,"valuation of zero");let v=0;while(n%BigInt(p)===0n){n/=BigInt(p);v++;}return v;};
  const vq=(a,p)=>a[0]===0n?Infinity:vp(a[0],p)-vp(a[1],p);
  const valuations=[2,3,5].map(p=>({p,minimum_binomial_coefficient_valuations:binomials.map(b=>Math.min(...b.map(a=>vq(a,p))))}));
  const searches=[];
  for(const p of [2,3,5]){
    let tested=0,admissible=0,failures=0,first=null,by_degree=[];
    for(let d=1;d<=12;d++){
      let a=0,f=0;
      for(let mask=0;mask<(1<<d);mask++){
        tested++;let ok=true,ones=0;
        for(let i=0;i<d;i++)ones+=(mask>>i)&1;
        for(let i=0;i+p<=d;i++){let sum=0;for(let j=0;j<p;j++)sum+=(mask>>(i+j))&1;if(sum===0){ok=false;break;}}
        if(!ok)continue;a++;admissible++;
        if(ones-vp(fact(d),p)<0){f++;failures++;if(first===null)first=d;}
      }
      by_degree.push({degree:d,admissible:a,failures:f});
    }
    searches.push({p,tested,admissible,failures,first_failure_degree:first,by_degree});
  }
  check(searches[0].admissible===984&&searches[0].failures===604&&searches[0].first_failure_degree===4,"p=2 weighted search");
  check(searches[1].admissible===3734&&searches[1].failures===30&&searches[1].first_failure_degree===9,"p=3 weighted search");
  check(searches[2].admissible===7172&&searches[2].failures===0,"p=5 weighted search");
  const sharp=[2,3,5].map(p=>({p,degree:p*p,rank:p*p+1,top_valuation:p-vp(fact(p*p),p)}));
  check(sharp.every(a=>a.top_valuation===-1),"all sharp top valuations");
  function Ring(modulus,nv,truncateIndex=-1){
    const m=BigInt(modulus),norm=c=>m?((c%m)+m)%m:c,zero=()=>new Map(),key=e=>e.join(",");
    function put(P,e,c){if(truncateIndex>=0&&e[truncateIndex]>=2)return;const k=key(e),v=norm((P.get(k)||0n)+c);if(v)P.set(k,v);else P.delete(k);}
    const constant=c=>{const P=zero();put(P,Array(nv).fill(0),BigInt(c));return P;};
    const variable=i=>{const P=zero(),e=Array(nv).fill(0);e[i]=1;put(P,e,1n);return P;};
    const add=(P,Q)=>{const R=new Map(P);for(const [k,c] of Q)put(R,k.split(",").map(Number),c);return R;};
    const scale=(P,c)=>{const R=zero();for(const [k,a] of P)put(R,k.split(",").map(Number),a*BigInt(c));return R;};
    const neg=P=>scale(P,-1);
    const sub=(P,Q)=>add(P,neg(Q));
    const mul=(P,Q)=>{const R=zero();for(const [k,a] of P)for(const [l,b] of Q){const e=k.split(",").map(Number),f=l.split(",").map(Number);put(R,e.map((v,i)=>v+f[i]),a*b);}return R;};
    const pow=(P,n)=>{let R=constant(1);for(let i=0;i<n;i++)R=mul(R,P);return R;};
    const eq=(P,Q)=>sub(P,Q).size===0;
    const display=(P,names)=>[...P].sort((a,b)=>{const e=a[0].split(",").map(Number),f=b[0].split(",").map(Number);for(let i=0;i<nv;i++)if(e[i]!==f[i])return f[i]-e[i];return 0;}).map(([k,c])=>String(c)+"*"+k.split(",").map((e,i)=>Number(e)?names[i]+"^"+e:"").filter(Boolean).join("*")).join(" + ")||"0";
    return {zero,constant,variable,add,sub,scale,mul,pow,eq,display};
  }
  const choose=(n,k)=>{let a=1;for(let i=1;i<=k;i++)a=a*(n-i+1)/i;check(Number.isInteger(a),"binomial integer");return a;};
  const cp=(R,p,a,b)=>{let P=R.zero();for(let i=1;i<p;i++)P=R.add(P,R.scale(R.mul(R.pow(a,i),R.pow(b,p-i)),choose(p,i)/p));return P;};
  const mod=(a,m)=>((a%m)+m)%m;
  const deformations=[],witt=[];
  for(const p of [2,3,5]){
    const Z=Ring(0,3),zx=Z.variable(0),zy=Z.variable(1),zz=Z.variable(2);
    const cocycle=Z.sub(Z.add(cp(Z,p,zx,zy),cp(Z,p,Z.add(zx,zy),zz)),Z.add(cp(Z,p,zy,zz),cp(Z,p,zx,Z.add(zy,zz))));
    check(cocycle.size===0,"integral cocycle p="+p);
    const R=Ring(p*p,3),x=R.variable(0),y=R.variable(1),z=R.variable(2);
    const law=(a,b)=>R.add(R.add(a,b),R.scale(cp(R,p,a,b),p));
    check(R.eq(law(law(x,y),z),law(x,law(y,z))),"symbolic associativity p="+p);
    const inverse=R.sub(R.scale(x,-1),R.scale(cp(R,p,x,R.scale(x,-1)),p));
    check(law(x,inverse).size===0&&law(inverse,x).size===0,"symbolic inverse p="+p);
    let multiple=R.zero();for(let i=0;i<p;i++)multiple=law(multiple,x);
    check(R.eq(multiple,R.scale(R.sub(x,R.pow(x,p)),p)),"symbolic p multiple p="+p);
    const F=Ring(p,1),t=F.variable(0),degrees=[];for(let d=1;d<=12;d*=p)degrees.push(d);
    let candidates=0,survivors=0;
    for(let code=0;code<p**degrees.length;code++){
      let q=code,B=F.zero(),coeffs=[],sum=0;
      for(const d of degrees){const c=q%p;q=Math.floor(q/p);coeffs.push(c);sum+=c;B=F.add(B,F.scale(F.pow(t,d),c));}
      if(sum%p!==1)continue;candidates++;
      if(F.sub(F.sub(B,F.pow(B,p)),F.scale(t,coeffs[0])).size===0)survivors++;
    }
    check(survivors===0,"no normalized additive survivors p="+p);
    const cpnum=(a,b)=>{let c=0;for(let i=1;i<p;i++)c+=(choose(p,i)/p)*a**i*b**(p-i);return c;};
    const star=(a,b)=>mod(a+b+p*cpnum(a,b),p*p);
    let finiteTriples=0;
    for(let a=0;a<p*p;a++)for(let b=0;b<p*p;b++)for(let c=0;c<p*p;c++){check(star(star(a,b),c)===star(a,star(b,c)),"finite deformation associativity");finiteTriples++;}
    let g=0,order=0;for(let i=1;i<=p*p;i++){g=star(g,1);if(g===0){order=i;break;}}
    check(order===p,"deformation section order");
    deformations.push({p,cocycle_integral:true,associativity_symbolic:true,inverse_symbolic:true,
      p_multiple:R.display(multiple,["T","U","V"]),finite_associativity_triples:finiteTriples,
      section_1_order:order,additive_degrees:degrees,normalized_candidates:candidates,survivors});
    const plus=(a,b)=>[mod(a[0]+b[0],p),mod(a[1]+b[1]-cpnum(a[0],b[0]),p)];
    const veq=(a,b)=>a[0]===b[0]&&a[1]===b[1],els=[];
    for(let a=0;a<p;a++)for(let b=0;b<p;b++)els.push([a,b]);
    let triples=0;for(const a of els)for(const b of els)for(const c of els){check(veq(plus(plus(a,b),c),plus(a,plus(b,c))),"Witt associativity");triples++;}
    let v=[0,0],atp=null,wittorder=0;for(let j=1;j<=p*p;j++){v=plus(v,[1,0]);if(j===p)atp=v.slice();if(veq(v,[0,0])){wittorder=j;break;}}
    check(veq(atp,[0,1])&&wittorder===p*p,"Witt section order");
    witt.push({p,p_times_1_0:atp,order:wittorder,associativity_triples:triples,wound_coefficient_t_valuation:1-p,
      valuation_not_divisible_by_p:mod(1-p,p)!==0});
  }
  const D=Ring(0,3,2),t=D.variable(0),u=D.variable(1),epsilon=D.variable(2),one=D.constant(1);
  const diagonal=v=>D.add(one,D.mul(epsilon,v));
  const twiceUpper=v=>D.add(D.scale(v,2),D.mul(epsilon,D.sub(D.pow(v,2),v)));
  check(D.eq(diagonal(D.add(t,u)),D.mul(diagonal(t),diagonal(u))),"dual diagonal group law");
  check(D.eq(twiceUpper(D.add(t,u)),D.add(D.mul(diagonal(t),twiceUpper(u)),twiceUpper(t))),"dual upper group law");
  check(D.eq(diagonal(one),D.add(one,epsilon))&&D.eq(twiceUpper(one),D.constant(2)),"dual value at one");
  return {signed_counterexample:signed,signed_family:{tested:7*12*7*9,witnesses:witnesses.length,

    rank_examples,formal_exponential_equals_binomial_through_degree:12,binomial_valuations:valuations,
    weighted_searches:searches,sharp_examples:sharp,deformations,witt,
    dual_numbers:{scalar_group_law:true,matrix_group_law:true,value_at_one:true},
    result:"ALL EXACT ARITHMETIC ASSERTIONS PASSED."};
}

"use strict";
function verifyEgroups(){
  const mod=(a,n)=>((a%n)+n)%n;
  const id=[0,0,0,0],rho=[-1,0,1,0],sigma=[0,0,0,1];
  function act(a,b,v){let [x,y]=v;if(b)[x,y]=[y,x];for(let i=0;i<a;i++)[x,y]=[-y,x-y];return [x,y];}
  function mul(g,h,n=0){const w=act(g[2],g[3],h),v=[g[0]+w[0],g[1]+w[1]];if(n)v.forEach((x,i)=>v[i]=mod(x,n));return [...v,mod(g[2]+(g[3]?-h[2]:h[2])+3*g[3]*h[3],6),(g[3]+h[3])%2];}
  function inv(g,n=0){for(let a=0;a<6;a++)for(let b=0;b<2;b++)if(mod(g[2]+(g[3]?-a:a)+3*g[3]*b,6)===0&&(g[3]+b)%2===0){const v=act(a,b,[-g[0],-g[1]]);if(n)v.forEach((x,i)=>v[i]=mod(x,n));return [...v,a,b];}}
  const key=g=>g.join(",");
  function generated(gs,n){const out=[id],seen=new Set([key(id)]);for(let i=0;i<out.length;i++)for(const g of gs){const h=mul(out[i],g,n),k=key(h);if(!seen.has(k)){seen.add(k);out.push(h);}}return out;}
  const prod=(...xs)=>xs.reduce((a,b)=>mul(a,b),id);
  const comm=(a,b,n)=>mul(mul(mul(a,b,n),inv(a,n),n),inv(b,n),n);
  function commsub(A,B,n){const gs=new Map();for(const a of A)for(const b of B){const c=comm(a,b,n);gs.set(key(c),c);}return generated([...gs.values()],n);}
  const orders=[1,2,3,4,8,9].map(n=>({n,generated:generated([rho,sigma],n).length,expected:12*n*n}));
  const G=generated([rho,sigma],2),derived=[G.length],lower=[G.length];let D=G,L=G;
  for(let i=0;i<3;i++){D=commsub(D,D,2);derived.push(D.length);L=commsub(G,L,2);lower.push(L.length);}
  const mm=(A,B)=>[mod(A[0]*B[0]+A[1]*B[2],4),mod(A[0]*B[1]+A[1]*B[3],4),mod(A[2]*B[0]+A[3]*B[2],4),mod(A[2]*B[1]+A[3]*B[3],4)];
  const mi=A=>[A[3],mod(-A[1],4),mod(-A[2],4),A[0]];
  const I=[1,0,0,1],r=[0,1,3,1],s=[0,3,1,0];
  function mg(gs){const out=[I],seen=new Set([key(I)]);for(let i=0;i<out.length;i++)for(const g of gs){const h=mm(out[i],g),k=key(h);if(!seen.has(k)){seen.add(k);out.push(h);}}return out;}
  const l0=[3,2,0,3],l1=[3,0,2,3],K=mg([l0,l1]),SL=mg([r,s]),ks=new Set(K.map(key));
  const normal=SL.every(g=>K.every(h=>ks.has(key(mm(mm(g,h),mi(g))))));
  const j=[3,0,0,1],jinverts=[l0,l1].every(l=>key(mm(mm(j,l),j))===key(mi(l)));
  const pc=(a,b)=>b.map(x=>a[x]),pi=a=>{const b=[];a.forEach((x,i)=>b[x]=i);return b;};
  const x=[1,0,2],y=[2,1,0],fy=pc(y,pc(pc(pc(x,y),pi(x)),pi(y)));
  function pg(gs){const out=[[0,1,2]],seen=new Set(["0,1,2"]);for(let i=0;i<out.length;i++)for(const g of gs){const h=pc(out[i],g),k=key(h);if(!seen.has(k)){seen.add(k);out.push(h);}}return out.length;}
  return {lambda0:prod(sigma,rho,inv(sigma),rho),lambda1:prod(rho,sigma,rho,inv(sigma)),rho_cubed:prod(rho,rho,rho),sigma_squared:prod(sigma,sigma),finite_generation:orders,derived_orders_mod2:derived,lower_central_orders_mod2:lower,mod4:{SL2_order:SL.length,K_order:K.length,K_normal:normal,index:SL.length/K.length,K,J_inverts_lambdas:jinverts},BISITE:{x,y,f_y:fy,original_image:pg([x,y]),composed_image:pg([x,fy])}};
}
const groupResult=verifyEgroups();
const equal=(a,b)=>JSON.stringify(a)===JSON.stringify(b);
if(!equal(groupResult.lambda0,[1,0,0,0])||!equal(groupResult.lambda1,[0,1,0,0]))throw Error("lattice marking");
if(!equal(groupResult.rho_cubed,groupResult.sigma_squared)||!equal(groupResult.rho_cubed,[0,0,3,0]))throw Error("modular relation");
if(!groupResult.finite_generation.every(x=>x.generated===x.expected))throw Error("generation");
if(!equal(groupResult.derived_orders_mod2,[48,12,4,1])||!equal(groupResult.lower_central_orders_mod2,[48,12,12,12]))throw Error("commutator series");
if(groupResult.mod4.SL2_order!==48||groupResult.mod4.K_order!==4||!groupResult.mod4.K_normal||groupResult.mod4.index!==12||!groupResult.mod4.J_inverts_lambdas)throw Error("mod-four subgroup");
if(groupResult.BISITE.original_image!==6||groupResult.BISITE.composed_image!==2||!equal(groupResult.BISITE.x,groupResult.BISITE.f_y))throw Error("BISITE");
const groupOutput=JSON.stringify(groupResult);
if(typeof text==="function")text(groupOutput);else console.log(groupOutput);
if(typeof store==="function")store("e_groups_replacement.out",groupOutput);

code/e_fermat.js
/* Exact Schreier graph checks for F2 -> (Z/n)^2. No dependencies. */
function reduceWord(word) {
  const result=[];
  for (const letter of word) {
    if(result.length && result[result.length-1]===-letter) result.pop();
    else result.push(letter);
  }
  return result;
}
function inverseWord(w) { return w.slice().reverse().map(a=>-a); }
function displayWord(w) {
  if(!w.length) return "1";
  return w.map(a=>({1:"x","-1":"x^-1",2:"y","-2":"y^-1"})[a]).join(" ");
}
function quotientWord(w,n) {
  let a=0,b=0;
  for(const z of w) {
    if(Math.abs(z)===1) a+=Math.sign(z); else b+=Math.sign(z);
  }
  return [((a%n)+n)%n,((b%n)+n)%n];
}
function checkFermat(n) {
  const key=v=>v.join(",");
  const reps=new Map([["0,0",[]]]);
  const queue=[[0,0]];
  const tree=new Set();
  for(let i=0;i<queue.length;i++) {
    const v=queue[i], r=reps.get(key(v));
    for(const z of [1,2]) {
      const t=[(v[0]+(z===1?1:0))%n,(v[1]+(z===2?1:0))%n];
      if(!reps.has(key(t))) {
        reps.set(key(t),r.concat(z)); queue.push(t);
        tree.add(key(v)+":"+z);
      }
    }
  }
  const generators=[];
  for(const v of queue) for(const z of [1,2]) {
    const t=[(v[0]+(z===1?1:0))%n,(v[1]+(z===2?1:0))%n];
    const w=reduceWord(reps.get(key(v)).concat([z],inverseWord(reps.get(key(t)))));
    if(tree.has(key(v)+":"+z)) {
      if(w.length) throw new Error("tree word not trivial");
    } else {
      if(!w.length) throw new Error("non-tree word trivial");
      const q=quotientWord(w,n);
      if(q[0]||q[1]) throw new Error("generator not in kernel");
      generators.push(w);
    }
  }
  if(reps.size!==n*n || tree.size!==n*n-1 || generators.length!==n*n+1)
    throw new Error("Euler/Schreier count failed");
  return {n,vertices:reps.size,positive_edges:2*n*n,tree_edges:tree.size,
          kernel_rank:generators.length,generators_n2:n===2?generators.map(displayWord):undefined};
}
const checks=Array.from({length:12},(_,i)=>checkFermat(i+1));
const lines=checks.map(o=>"n="+o.n+" vertices="+o.vertices+" edges="+o.positive_edges+
  " spanning_tree="+o.tree_edges+" kernel_rank="+o.kernel_rank);
lines.push("n=2 Schreier basis: "+checks[1].generators_n2.join(" ; "));
lines.push("ALL SCHREIER GRAPH ASSERTIONS PASSED.");
const rendered=lines.join("\n")+"\n";
if(typeof text==="function") text(rendered); else console.log(rendered);
if(typeof store==="function") store("E_fermat_output",rendered);

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Additional exact checks: Witt vectors, wound groups, and derived length."""
from math import comb
from itertools import product
import sympy as s
print('Witt-vector additive law: W_2(F_p).')
for p in [2,3,5]:
 def plus(a,b):
  return ((a[0]+b[0])%p,(a[1]+b[1]-sum((comb(p,i)//p)*a[0]**i*b[0]**(p-i) for i in range(1,p)))%p)
 allv=list(product(range(p),repeat=2))
 assert all(plus(plus(a,b),c)==plus(a,plus(b,c)) for a,b,c in product(allv,repeat=3))
 v=(0,0);atp=None;order=None
 for n in range(1,p*p+1):
  v=plus(v,(1,0))
  if n==p:atp=v
  if v==(0,0):order=n;break
 assert atp==(0,1) and order==p*p
 print('p=',p,'p*(1,0)=',atp,'order=',order,'associativity on',len(allv)**3,'triples: PASS')

print('\nWound one-dimensional group over F_p(t): y^p=x-t^(1-p)x^p; g=(t,0).')
t=s.symbols('t')
for p in [2,3,5]:
 assert s.expand(t-t**(1-p)*t**p)==0
 assert (1-p)%p!=0
 print('p=',p,'g lies on group: PASS; valuation of coefficient=',1-p,'not divisible by p: PASS')

print('\nDerived length of the order-48 quotient (Z/2)^2 semidirect Dic3.')
R=((0,1),(1,1));S=((0,1),(1,0));I=((1,0),(0,1))
def matmul(A,B):return tuple(tuple(sum(A[i][k]*B[k][j] for k in range(2))%2 for j in range(2)) for i in range(2))
def hm(a,b):return ((a[0]+(-1)**a[1]*b[0]+3*a[1]*b[1])%6,(a[1]+b[1])%2)
def power(A,n):
 C=I
 for _ in range(n):C=matmul(C,A)
 return C
act={(a,b):matmul(power(R,a),power(S,b)) for a,b in product(range(6),range(2))}
e=(0,0,0,0)
def mul(u,v):
 A=act[u[2:]];h=hm(u[2:],v[2:])
 return ((u[0]+A[0][0]*v[0]+A[0][1]*v[1])%2,(u[1]+A[1][0]*v[0]+A[1][1]*v[1])%2,*h)
G=list(product(range(2),range(2),range(6),range(2)))
inv={a:next(b for b in G if mul(a,b)==e) for a in G}
def subgroup(gens):
 seen={e};todo=[e]
 while todo:
  a=todo.pop()
  for b in gens:
   c=mul(a,b)
   if c not in seen:seen.add(c);todo.append(c)
 return seen
series=[set(G)]
while len(series[-1])>1:
 A=series[-1];comms={mul(mul(mul(a,b),inv[a]),inv[b]) for a,b in product(A,repeat=2)}
 series.append(subgroup(comms))
print('Derived subgroup orders:',[len(A) for A in series])
assert [len(A) for A in series]==[48,12,4,1]
print('Thus S is not metabelian in the modern derived-length-two sense.')

lower=[set(G)]
for _ in range(3):
 A=lower[-1]
 comms={mul(mul(mul(a,b),inv[a]),inv[b]) for a,b in product(G,A)}
 lower.append(subgroup(comms))
print('Lower central subgroup orders through gamma_4:',[len(A) for A in lower])
assert [len(A) for A in lower]==[48,12,12,12]

print('\nSmooth Ga deformations over every Z/p^2, checked at p=2,3,5.')
x,y,z,T=s.symbols('x y z T')
def reduce_poly(f,mod,*vars):
 return s.Poly(s.expand(f),*vars).trunc(mod).as_expr()
for p in [2,3,5]:
 C=sum((comb(p,i)//p)*x**i*y**(p-i) for i in range(1,p))
 def cp(a,b):return C.subs({x:a,y:b},simultaneous=True)
 def law(a,b,*vars):return reduce_poly(a+b+p*cp(a,b),p*p,*vars)
 assert s.expand(cp(x,y)+cp(x+y,z)-cp(y,z)-cp(x,y+z))==0
 assoc=reduce_poly(law(law(x,y,x,y),z,x,y,z)-law(x,law(y,z,y,z),x,y,z),p*p,x,y,z)
 assert assoc==0
 inverse=reduce_poly(-x-p*cp(x,-x),p*p,x)
 assert law(x,inverse,x)==0 and law(inverse,x,x)==0
 multiple=s.Integer(0)
 for _ in range(p):multiple=law(multiple,T,T)
 expected=reduce_poly(p*(T-T**p),p*p,T)
 assert s.expand(multiple-expected)==0
 assert int(multiple.subs(T,1))%(p*p)==0
 exponents=[];degree=1
 while degree<=12:exponents.append(degree);degree*=p
 candidates=0;survivors=[]
 for coeffs in product(range(p),repeat=len(exponents)):
  B=sum(c*T**d for c,d in zip(coeffs,exponents))
  if int(B.subs(T,1))%p!=1:continue
  candidates+=1
  condition=reduce_poly(B-B**p-coeffs[0]*T,p,T)
  if condition==0:survivors.append(coeffs)
 assert not survivors
 print('p=',p,'C_p=',C,'inverse=',inverse,'[p]_G(T)=',multiple,
       '; cocycle, associativity, inverse: PASS; normalized additive candidates degree<=12=',
       candidates,'survivors=',len(survivors))

print('\nCharacteristic-zero nilpotent base Q[epsilon]/(epsilon^2).')
eps,U=s.symbols('epsilon U')
def dual(f):return s.rem(s.Poly(s.expand(f),eps),s.Poly(eps**2,eps)).as_expr()
assert dual((1+eps*T)*(1+eps*U)-(1+eps*(T+U)))==0
g=s.Matrix([[1+eps,1],[0,1]])
N=g-s.eye(2)
X=(N-N*N/2).applyfunc(dual)
phi=(s.eye(2)+T*X+T*T*X*X/2).applyfunc(dual)
assert (phi.subs(T,T+U)-phi*phi.subs(T,U)).applyfunc(dual)==s.zeros(2)
assert (phi.subs(T,1)-g).applyfunc(dual)==s.zeros(2)
print('Scalar section 1+epsilon: phi(T)=1+epsilon*T; group law: PASS')
print('Matrix g=',g,'; log(g)=',X,'; phi(T)=',phi)
print('Matrix homomorphism law and value at 1: PASS')
print('\nALL ADDITIONAL ASSERTIONS PASSED.')

report.md
| Task or lead | Outcome | Statements and precise qualification |
|---|---|---|
| A.a — complete dictionary | unfinished | A.1 and A.11 give the core and typed formal dictionaries. A.10 records the unrecovered, incomplete late concordance. |
| A.b — every formula and reading | unfinished | The core universal identities, exceptional fibres, finite counts, and the C4 finer charts are proved and computed. The requirement to check every late formula is not met. |
| A.c — canonical pinning | proved | A.2–A.3 construct the matrix units over the stated bases and prove uniqueness as an algebra pinning. |
| A.d — modular triples | proved | A.7–A.8 classify the GL and SL orbits over the requested bases and compute every requested finite test. |
| B.a — functor and equations | proved | B.1 and D.1 give the exact scan399 functor over every ring, including nilpotents. |
| B.b — geometry and exceptional fibres | proved | B.2–B.6 prove flat dimension6, smoothness precisely away from2, the nonreduced fibre at2, and the smooth four-component fibre at3. The finer C4 scheme is B.23. |
| B.c — structure and exact sequences | partly settled | B.7–B.13 and B.17–B.22 prove the specified quotient, kernel, centre, stabilizer and reconstruction statements. The remaining late source-wide identifications, including the unverified scan690 epimorphism, remain A.10/B.21. |
| B.d — strict unipotent lifts | proved | A.4 determines the scalar ambiguity and gives a four-element-ring counterexample. |
| B.e — parametrization and missed points | proved | A.6, A.9 and B.14 treat the specified chart, actual norm conditions, and a counterexample to the weakened conditions. They do not identify that chart with full M. |
| B.f — finite counts | proved | B.12 and the A/B output cover all requested prime fields through13 and Z/4, Z/8, Z/9. |
| B.g — general signed models and tower passage | partly settled | B.15 proves the general unit-v identities; B.16 computes the adelic quotient defect. B.17–B.22 prove conditional extension formulas. The complete global tower action remains unconstructed; D.3 refutes the literal cyclotomic target map. |
| C.a — actual presentation source | proved | C.1–C.2 identify the constant-factor fppf pushouts and integral amalgams. |
| C.b — target images | proved | C.3 proves the sheaf epimorphisms and determinant cokernel; C.6 supplies GL. |
| C.c — kernel | proved | C.4 refutes centrality; C.5 identifies the exact normal subgroup sheaf of Gauss relations. |
| C.d — ringwise tests | partly settled | C.7–C.10 prove all word orders, seven sheaf cardinalities, matrix counts, and localized target amalgams. C.11 leaves the nonsquare Z/4 lift and full integral/Artinian section descriptions. |
| C.e — residue characteristics2 and3 | proved | Constant factors remain distinct from roots of unity. C.5–C.6 give corrected presentations over both local bases, with full schematic mu2. |
| C.f — corrected theorem | proved | C.3–C.6 state and prove the corrected integral sheaf theorem; C.11 explicitly excludes the unresolved section questions. |
| D.a — arithmetic points | proved | D.1–D.2 determine integral, local integral, local field, profinite, and finite-adelic point groups. |
| D.b — Galois/GT comparisons | partly settled | D.3–D.7 provide refutations, finite equation counts, a noncongruence quotient, and an extra adelic point. The corrected global image/kernel and compatible GT lifting remain D.8. |
| D.c — central extensions | proved | D.9–D.10 classify the finite classes. D.16 classifies central mu2 extensions of the exact M over Qbar, Q, and Z. |
| D.d — Brauer classes | proved | D.11 gives the split literal symbol (-3,3); D.12 gives the distinct period-two symbol (-1,-3). D.15 supplies a dihedral realization. |
| D.e — class-field construction | proved | D.13 treats the constant imaginary-quadratic obstruction. D.14 proves the general constant Dic3 criterion and an explicit full-image d=37 example with ramification. Nonconstant cocycles are a separate interpretation. |
| D.f — generation | proved | D.17 proves the result for every modulus and checks1–48. The exact matrix targets are specified rather than guessing the scan's larger ambient notation. |
| D.g — motivic representations | partly settled | D.18–D.19 give precise positive realizations; D.20 refutes the unrestricted geometric-origin interpretation; D.21 gives the exact continuous descent criterion. No general motivic classification is claimed. |
| E.a — one-parameter subgroups | partly settled | E.1–E.4 give the characteristic-zero linear theorem, field descent, all-prime counterexamples, and integral criterion. Nonlinear nonfield existence remains E.27. |
| E.b — integral implications and searches | refuted | E.5–E.8 separate all coherent readings, give the genuine minimum-rank-five counterexample, prove the sharp p-squared bound, and execute the degree12 searches. |
| E.c — positive powers | partly settled | E.9–E.11 prove affine Z and noetherian closed linear cases. The stated non-affine instance over Spec Z remains unproved. |
| E.d — envelope and comparisons | partly settled | E.12–E.17 construct the envelope, prove its stated universal range, compute fibres and comparison maps, and refute full fppf exactness. The unrestricted general-base affine target case remains E.27. |
| E.e — modular example and actions | partly settled | E.18–E.20 and E.26 compute S and its models; B.23 computes the finer parameter scheme; E.28 computes finite Fermat relative kernels. The full sign-consistent external action remains E.21. |
| E.f — BISITE and completions | refuted | E.22 proves the completion-preserving endomorphism test. E.23 refutes descent of the specified Pi0 S3 cover and distinguishes the retained mod2 cover. |
| E.g — two rational theories | proved | E.24–E.25 give exact theorem reductions and explicit separating representations. |
| Item1 | partly settled | The exact group scheme and its arithmetic are extensively determined, but the complete late source-wide dictionary and all tower identifications are unfinished. |
| Item2 | partly settled | The corrected presentation theorem is proved; C.11 retains the exact section-lifting residual. |
| Item3 | partly settled | The retained heading assigns Items3 and4 collectively to Part D. Their separate opening partition is not reconstructed or invented. D.8 and the general D.g scope remain unfinished. |
| Item4 | partly settled | Same collective Part D coverage and explicit partition-recovery limitation as Item3. Central extensions and the corrected constant embedding problem are proved. |
| Item5 | partly settled | E.1–E.28 give the integral and completion results; E.11, E.21 and E.27 are exact remaining questions. |
| Lead1 — bare presentation/central kernel | refuted | C.4 gives a noncentral kernel on every nonempty base; C.5–C.6 provide exact replacements. |
| Lead2 — norm-one identification | refuted | The full unit/norm coordinates and determinant fibres cannot be discarded. B.1–B.9 give the corrected group and exceptional fibres. |
| Lead3 — completion identification | refuted | E.14–E.17 and E.26 distinguish the integral envelope from full algebraic, ordinary Malcev, a single relative, divided-power, and Hall/BCH models. |
| Lead4 — Brauer-symbol comparison | proved | The literal and cyclic-four classes are separated: orders1 and2, respectively; the middle group's order4 is not a Brauer period. |
| After LM3 — Galois image | partly settled | The literal cyclotomic map is impossible, and no continuous Galois image in full M is open. A corrected global target/source and image/kernel theorem remain. |
| After LM3 — GT-hat | partly settled | Finite relations and actual identity/conjugation pairs are proved. A compatible additional global element and exact corrected image/kernel are not. |
| After LM3 — GT0-hat | partly settled | Finite two-cycle/three-cycle shadows are computed. No strict global comparison with GT-hat is inferred from them. |
| After LM3 — genus-zero/genus-one tower passage | partly settled | B.16 exposes the central quotient obstruction; D.3 blocks the literal cyclotomic interpretation. The corrected global tower action is not supplied. |
| After BISITE — Stallings completion test | proved | E.22 proves all asserted lower-central, pro-prime, and rational Malcev consequences directly, with finite S3 obstruction. |
| After BISITE — specified S3 covering | refuted | E.23 proves the indicated Pi0 representation cannot descend to S. The ordinary SL2 mod2 representation is different and retained. |
| GALOIS C.13–C.14 — rational theories | proved | E.24–E.25 identify the discrete, proalgebraic, and envelope objects and their representation differences. |
| L5 — finite relative quotient | proved | E.20 gives the canonical abelian-kernel relative quotient for SL2Z to Dic3, with the GL order24 distinction retained. |
| LM7 Fermat tower — discrete finite quotient | proved | E.28 gives free prounipotent kernel rank n-squared-plus-one, with Schreier computations1–12. |
| LM8 Lie/relative arithmetic action | partly settled | The discrete free-Lie relative calculation is proved. The arithmetic quotient involving S3 times Zhat-units and the full marked external action remain E.21/E.28. |
| Rules5 — low orientable types and centres | proved | F.1 covers(0,0),(0,1),(0,2),(1,0), retaining their entire centres and computing their envelopes. |
| Rules5 — (0,3) versus(1,1) | proved | Both have centre-free F2, with three peripheral classes versus one commutator peripheral class. This is not an unqualified reconstruction theorem after forgetting markings. |
| Rules5 — residue characteristics2 and3 | proved | B.4–B.6, C.5–C.8, E.2–E.3, E.14, and E.19 treat the relevant exceptional schemes and points explicitly. |
| Rules5 — real and non-orientable test cases | partly settled | F.2 gives the modular orientation extension; F.3 gives the nonsplit integral Klein-bottle model. D.13 gives the real-place obstruction. |
| Rules5 — all real/non-orientable hyperbolic comparisons | unfinished | No general comparison theorem for arbitrary real arithmetic actions or all non-orientable hyperbolic surfaces is established. |

# Reconstruction report

## Delivery status

This is a substantial but incomplete research deliverable. The source has 97 numbered statements, with a separate row for each in results.csv, and 57 source-reading records in readings.csv. “Open” means unproved in this work; it does not claim that a question is open in the literature. The explicit unfinished concordance and the unfinished broad comparison rows prevent a claim that the whole assignment is complete.

The execution environment disconnected at approximately 19:31:38 UTC on November11,2026. Repeated recovery attempts continued for more than ten minutes and much longer thereafter. A retry at20:22:47 UTC still returned “No usable execution environment is available.” A direct attempt to upload an already existing source file also failed because no primary environment was available.

The existing workspace copies are inaccessible. Their deletion has not been established. No final combined paper PDF, final ZIP, or successful persistent upload is claimed. An earlier D-only TeX check succeeded before disconnection; it is not a compilation or visual validation of the reconstructed complete paper.

The user's explicit disconnection fallback is therefore followed by delivering full text blocks for the main LaTeX file, all seven section inputs, the combined registers, bibliography, this report, the recovered Python source, six complete newly executed JavaScript programs and their outputs, and an unexecuted local build helper. The reconstructed prose has been edited for consistency; it is not a verbatim recovery of inaccessible source bytes.

## Principal mathematical conclusions

The exact scan399 scheme is the six-dimensional determinant-constrained product using the commutative Eisenstein unit group and the signed Gaussian normalizer. It is flat over Z, smooth away from2, and nonreduced at2 with multiplicity4. Characteristic3 is a degeneration of the quadratic algebra but not a singular fibre of full M. The connected sign kernel M00 is smooth over every prime. The finer C4 scan41 parameter scheme has a different dimension and is smooth over all Z.

The normalized matrix problem has an explicit integral pinning. Its canonical algebra isomorphism must not be confused with arbitrary automorphisms of a general-linear group. Strict projective unipotent lifting has a scalar infinitesimal ambiguity at2. The wording of A.2 is corrected to the unique line on which the operator acts pointwise as identity; other invariant lines can occur over nonreduced rings.

The proposed constant-cyclic/additive presentation is a genuine fppf pushout, but its bare relation has a noncentral kernel. A reduced matrix-identity word and its nontrivial commutator survive sheafification. Nine explicit Gauss relation families give the exact signed kernel. Projectivization requires the full schematic mu2, and a multiplicative determinant factor supplies GL2. Finite word groups and actual sheaf sections differ sharply, including at F3, F5 and Z/4.

The central double-cover problem for the precise M is solved. With kernel and quotient fixed, there are32 classes over Qbar, a group F2^4 plus two rational square-class parameters over Q, and exactly8 classes over Z. The integral representatives adjoin square roots of delta^a e^b t^c. The proof uses the connected component's Kummer classes, uniqueness of its pointed splitting, affine descent, connectedness of the total mu2-squared scheme over Z, exact Cartier duality, and Minkowski's unramified-extension theorem. These are marked extension classes, not a claim of eight pairwise nonisomorphic abstract middle groups.

The literal cup product on C3-611 is(-3,3)=0. The different constant-C4 obstruction is(-1,-3), of period2, ramified at3 and infinity. A constant Dic3 lift of an imaginary quadratic character is impossible. More generally a full constant Dic3 lift with quadratic field Q(sqrt(d)) exists exactly when(d,-1)=0. Anti-invariant cubic ray characters show that the S3 part always exists. An explicit example uses d=37, the cubic X^3-X^2-3X+1, and a quartic subfield of Q(zeta37), with all stated local degrees computed.

For the literal full M target, a cyclotomic-determinant Galois homomorphism is impossible already modulo5. Without any determinant assumption, every continuous Galois image in this target is nonopen by the split torus at7. The finite GT equation counts and the noncongruence quotient SL2(F8) do not produce the missing global GT automorphism.

The integral exponentiation claims require their actual hypotheses. There is a smooth obstruction over every Z/p² even with a geometrically additive section of orderp. Over torsion-free linear bases, integral divided powers of log(g) are necessary and sufficient. With all geometric-fibre hypotheses retained, rank5 is the first integral matrix failure; weights1,6,1,6 give top coefficient3/2. The local nilpotency bound p² is sharp. Positive powers nevertheless work for every affine finitely presented group scheme over Z.

The envelope is an inverse limit of finite lattice models. Its rational kernel is a vector group and its positive-characteristic fibres are constant profinite completions. Its map to the profinite constant quotient is fpqc but need not be an fppf epimorphism; an explicit transcendental p-adic coordinate proves this. Finite relative completion, full proalgebraic completion, ordinary Malcev completion, integer-valued polynomial models, divided-power models, and rank-one Hall/BCH models are distinguished by proved maps and counterexamples.

The requested modular quotient is Z² semidirect Dic3 and is not metabelian in the modern sense. Its full envelope and its finite model have different adelic point groups and fibres. Its nilpotent reflection is C12 and its rational Malcev completion is trivial. The specific BISITE S3 cover of Pi0 is lost upon abelianizing Pi0; the ordinary mod2 modular cover is a different retained map. The discrete Fermat relative kernel has free rank n²+1, giving abelian-kernel dimension5 at n=2, incompatible with the indicated two- and six-dimensional models.

## Computational evidence and its limits

Six replacement JavaScript programs were actually executed after the outage in the surviving V8 runtime. They require no third-party packages.

- ab_reconstructed.js checks universal Laurent-polynomial identities, all requested M and modular-triple counts, the finite parametrization cases, the finer parameter charts, and explicit weakening/lift counterexamples.
- c_replacement.js performs finite coset enumeration, checks the complete generator permutations, enumerates every matrix over the seven finite rings, lists every missed projective word-image class, and checks all2880 words in the stated quadratic-etale descent family. Root reran this source; its delivered output is authoritative.
- d_reconstructed.js gives19 output groups: finite GT shadows with automorphism checks, normalized cochains, finite central extensions, all504 elements of SL2(F8), normal closures, the norm obstruction, Hensel residues, ramification checks, the elliptic point count, period polynomial, and generation for every n from1 through48.
- e_arithmetic_replacement.js uses exact rational and modular arithmetic. It checks8190 weighted words per prime at2,3,5;5292 signed-family matrices; degree12 independent polynomial recurrences; smooth deformations; Witt laws; and dual-number identities.
- e_groups_replacement.js verifies the lattice marking, finite quotients, the normal subgroup modulo4, derived and lower central series, and the S3 image collapse.
- e_fermat.js verifies all Schreier graph counts for1 through12 and prints the rank-five basis at n=2. Root reran it after correcting an intermediate transmission error.

These computations are finite certificates of the stated finite claims. They do not prove global GT lifting, arbitrary fppf descent, or the unbounded Z/4 section question. Infinite-group, integral, and universal statements have separate proofs in the paper.

The original full A/B Groebner and Jacobian logs were executed before the outage but are inaccessible. The new A/B program does not purport to reproduce every assertion in those lost logs. The monic-basis and geometric arguments remain written in Part B.

## Exact remaining mathematical work

1. A.10: complete the late source concordance and check every remaining formula, including the claimed late epimorphism. Core conditional reconstruction identities do not replace this source task.
2. C.11: decide whether[diag(3,1)] lifts through A_p(Z/4). The full image is24 or48. The executed2880-word family finds64 fixed words and only four square-determinant targets; arbitrary lengths and arbitrary fppf covers remain.
3. D.8: formulate and construct the corrected global target or source extension, then prove compatible lifting, all defining GT relations, and exact image/kernel assertions. A noncongruence element is not an automorphism.
4. E.11: extend the positive-power theorem to the stated non-affine target case.
5. E.21: construct the full sign-consistent external action and the arithmetic relative-completion comparison.
6. E.27: prove or refute nonlinear existence over nonfield Q-algebras and the unrestricted noetherian-base affine-target envelope property.
7. The general real and non-orientable hyperbolic comparison remains outside the proved explicit cases.

Other intermediate metadata, source contact sheets, and temporary build files are also inaccessible. The combined CSV and bibliography supplied here are reconstructed registers, not recovered original bytes.
